```text
1	\section{Introduction}
2	\label{sec:intro}
3	
4	The text on which large language models are trained increasingly contains their own outputs.
5	As synthetic content proliferates across the web, a recursive loop emerges: models trained on web corpora ingest text generated by earlier models, and their outputs feed into the next generation's training data \cite{shumailov2024_nature,alemohammad2024_mad}.
6	This model$\to$data$\to$model cycle is no longer hypothetical; it is already occurring in the wild. Yet whether the recursive generation depth of a text can be estimated from its statistical properties remains unstudied, a question that matters because first-generation synthetic text may be benign or even beneficial for training, while deeply recursive text drives the distributional collapse that degrades model quality \cite{dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails}.
7	
8	Existing tools address adjacent but distinct problems.
9	Binary detectors such as Binoculars \cite{binoculars2024}, Fast-DetectGPT \cite{fast_detectgpt2024}, and DivEye \cite{diveye2026} classify text as human-written or machine-generated, collapsing all depths $d \geq 1$ into a single category.
10	Watermarking \cite{kirchenbauer2023_watermark} requires proactive embedding and cannot be applied retroactively.
11	The model collapse literature \cite{shumailov2024_nature,dohmatob2024_tale_of_tails} assumes depth is known a priori; \citet{drayson2025_detection_prevents_collapse} filter synthetic data via binary detection without distinguishing depths.
12	No existing method estimates the ordinal recursive generation depth of a text passage from its content alone.
13	
14	We find that recursive generation leaves quantifiable statistical fingerprints in text, and these fingerprints are \textbf{consistent in direction but scale-dependent in magnitude} across the three model families and three decoding strategies studied.
15	Specifically, perplexity tail statistics (the 90th-percentile perplexity, \texttt{p90\_ppl}, and surprisal variance, \texttt{var\_surprisal}) emerge as dominant depth indicators, ranking in the top two features by importance in 9/9 and 8/9 model--strategy configurations, respectively.
16	Instruction-tuned variants exhibit monotonically decreasing AUC (regularization), whereas base models can show non-monotonic trajectories; instruction tuning thus acts as a regularizer on the depth signal rather than a source of degradation.
17	
18	This paper is an empirical study and problem formalization rather than an algorithmic contribution.
19	We formalize recursive generation depth estimation as ordinal classification and introduce a lightweight pipeline: compute 19 reference-model statistical features (effective dimensionality ${\approx}$4--5 due to multicollinearity; \S\ref{sec:atlas:features}) and classify with a Random Forest into $K{+}1$ depth classes.
20	To characterize how recursive generation reshapes these features, we construct the \emph{Depth Degradation Atlas}: a $3 \times 3$ grid of model families $\times$ decoding strategies spanning 4 depths (180K samples).
21	The atlas reveals strategy-dependent degradation trajectories and we introduce the \emph{Effective Discriminable Depth} (EDD) metric to quantify how far into the recursion chain adjacent depths remain distinguishable.
22	
23	Our contributions are:
24	\begin{itemize}[nosep,leftmargin=*]
25	    \item \textbf{Task formalization and benchmark.} We define recursive generation depth estimation as ordinal classification, introduce the EDD metric, and construct a 180K-sample benchmark ($3$ model families $\times$ $3$ decoding strategies $\times$ $4$ depths).
26	
27	    \item \textbf{Depth Degradation Atlas.} Perplexity tail statistics dominate depth discrimination (top-2 in 9/9 and 8/9 cells); $\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}4$ for all three families, validated by 5-class (d0--d4) extension on three families (71--80\%); at the stricter $T{=}0.75$, Pythia achieves $p{=}0.001$ with graded confidence across families in the d3--d4 region.
28	
29	    \item \textbf{Generalization and cross-strategy analysis.} Cross-model LOO 66.4\% (d1--d3: 55.4\%), cross-seed replication across three families, prompted 69.5\%, cross-scale 7--14B (54--70\%, attenuating with scale), cross-domain arXiv 74.1\%. Cross-strategy transfer (59.1\%) reveals a structural difficulty: different decoding strategies induce qualitatively distinct degradation trajectories, with d1/d2 boundary inversion under strategy mismatch. Surface-text controls (zero-shot LLMs 30--36\%) and binary detection/membership inference baselines (DetectGPT, Min-K\% Prob: 30--33\%) confirm the signal resides in distributional statistics inaccessible from surface text or detection scores.
30	\end{itemize}
```

</details>

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_012KJkXTtN9QcJiwisYRLDa3`</summary>

```text
1	\section*{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	\paragraph{Cross-strategy deployment constraint.}
5	Cross-strategy transfer induces a d1/d2 boundary inversion under temperature sampling.
6	Per-strategy z-score normalization partially corrects the inversion under logistic regression (GPT-2~XL d1v2 AUC $0.24{\to}0.56$, OLMo $0.19{\to}0.70$) but yields negligible improvement for the Random Forest classifier used throughout the main experiments ($\Delta < 0.02$\,pp), and even in the best case (logistic regression) leaves a residual gap relative to within-strategy performance (${\sim}0.83$); a two-stage pipeline (strategy identification + strategy-conditioned classification) is therefore needed for reliable cross-strategy deployment.
7	
8	\paragraph{Strategy-unknown deployment.}
9	In strategy-unknown settings, naive transfer on d1--d3 yields only 45.9\%, and d1/d2 predictions may invert.
10	A two-stage pipeline recovers 11.5\,pp over LOO, though per-model gains vary (GPT-2~XL $+$16.4\,pp vs.\ Pythia $+$8.0\,pp).
11	Practical deployment requires either strategy metadata or learned routing.
12	
13	\paragraph{d0 inflation of headline metrics.}
14	Depth~0 (human-written text) is near-perfectly separable (recall $>$97\% across all nine model--strategy settings), inflating headline accuracy.
15	Cross-model transfer accuracy of 66.4\% is substantially driven by d0; d2 recall drops to 26.8\% in the worst case (Pythia holdout).
16	
17	\paragraph{Controlled vs.\ ecological setting.}
18	The primary benchmark uses Wikipedia seed text with controlled LoRA fine-tuning (1--1.5B models), a design that isolates the depth variable but diverges from wild contamination where models train on mixed corpora with prior-generation outputs at unknown proportions.
19	Domain bias controls confirm depth as the dominant factor: LOGO accuracy (72.1\%) matches standard CV (72.0\%), seed-group prediction is at chance (10.2\%; Appendix~\ref{app:domain_bias}).
20	Cross-scale experiments (7--14B, \S\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}) show signal that attenuates with scale; at 14B, d0/d1 separability nearly vanishes ($\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$) while d1--d3 discrimination persists; this constitutes a boundary condition for the current feature set (confounded by self-reference model; Appendix~\ref{app:cross_scale_confounds}).
21	A base-model control suggests instruction-tuning regularizes degradation.
22	Segment-level estimation in mixed-depth documents remains open (Appendix~\ref{app:mixed_depth}); in particular, partial mixing (accumulation), where a single document contains fragments from different recursive depths, is a key open question, as our framework assumes uniform depth per passage.
23	
24	\paragraph{Domain-dependent false positive rate.}
25	On editorial text (WikiText-103), all classifiers achieve $\geq$98.5\% d0 accuracy, but on web-crawl data (C4, 5{,}000 passages), per-model FPR ranges from 4.7\% (OLMo) to 22.4\% (GPT-2~XL).
26	A pooled RF classifier evaluated on larger corpora (10{,}000 passages each) yields 34\% FPR on C4 and 17\% on FineWeb \cite{penedo2024_fineweb}; the gap reflects quality filtering in FineWeb removing feature-anomalous text that triggers false positives.
27	Misclassifications concentrate in template/SEO content with elevated repetition features (\texttt{rep\_3gram} $+$37\%, \texttt{rep\_4gram} $+$75\% vs.\ correctly classified d0).
28	Confidence thresholding ($P(\mathrm{d0}) \geq 0.15$) reduces pooled FPR from 34\%/17\% to 2.8\%/0.7\% on C4/FineWeb at minimal accuracy cost ($-$0.3\,pp); high FPR is therefore a decision-threshold problem, not a fundamental calibration defect, though the optimal threshold remains domain-dependent (Appendix~\ref{app:calibration}).
29	
30	\paragraph{Adversarial robustness.}
31	Adversarial paraphrasing \cite{sadasivan2023_paraphrase} could disrupt the perplexity tail features that drive classification; depth estimation's robustness under such attacks is untested.
32	
33	\paragraph{EDD monotonicity assumption.}
34	Our EDD definition (Eq.~\ref{eq:edd}) requires all pairwise AUCs up to depth $d^*$ to exceed $T$, which is well-behaved when AUC decays monotonically, as observed in all our configurations.
35	However, \citet{bakshi2026_collapse_improvement} show that early recursion can temporarily \emph{improve} model quality, which could produce non-monotonic AUC profiles (e.g., a dip at d1/d2 followed by recovery at d2/d3).
36	Our cross-strategy transfer results provide an empirical example: training on nucleus + top-$k$ and testing on temperature yields d1-vs-d2 AUC as low as 0.129 (GPT-2~XL) and 0.253 (OLMo), violating the monotonicity assumption.
37	In such cases EDD would conservatively report a lower value than the deepest discriminable pair, potentially underestimating the true discriminable range; extending EDD to handle non-contiguous discriminable regions is future work.
38	The choice of threshold $T$ is also application-dependent: at $T{=}0.80$, several cells lose one depth level relative to $T{=}0.75$.
39	
40	\paragraph{Reference model dependency.}
41	Reference model choice affects classification accuracy by up to 11\,pp; same-family references yield optimal performance, though all configurations exceed $2.5\times$ chance.
42	Surprisal-based features are computed relative to Pythia-1.4B, which coincides with one of the three generators.
43	For the Pythia pipeline this constitutes self-model feature extraction, while OLMo and GPT-2~XL measurements are cross-model.
44	Empirically, the self-model configuration does not yield the highest accuracy (Pythia 78.7\% vs.\ GPT-2~XL 85.9\%); the overlap therefore does not confer a systematic advantage.
45	An ablation with OLMo-1B and GPT-2 XL as alternative references shows accuracy ranging from 67--79\% across three reference architectures (Appendix~\ref{app:ref_ablation}), all exceeding $2.5\times$ chance; same-family reference yields optimal performance ($\Delta{\leq}$11\,pp).
46	The choice of reference model affects feature values; a different reference model may shift absolute feature magnitudes and potentially alter discriminability at depth boundaries.
47	Reference-free surface features alone achieve 53.7\% (partial signal in lexical statistics) but trail the full pipeline by 25pp; surprisal-based features are therefore essential.
48	Simple multi-reference ensembling does not outperform the best single reference (73.7\% vs.\ 78.6\%).
49	
50	\paragraph{Single LoRA instance.}
51	Cross-seed replication covers 3 of 9 cells (Pythia, OLMo, GPT-2~XL nucleus); seed=43 yields $+$0.1 to $+$3.2\,pp higher accuracy, with 2/3 deltas statistically significant (non-overlapping CIs).
52	Feature rankings and depth ordering are consistent across seeds, but single-seed absolute accuracy carries ${\sim}$1--3\,pp uncertainty; cross-cell comparisons should be interpreted cautiously.
53	
54	\paragraph{Additional caveats.}
55	Depth signal scales with passage length (68.4\% at 128 tokens, 77.8\% at 512; Appendix~\ref{app:length_ablation}), saturating near 500 tokens.
56	Prompted generation was validated on only 1 of 9 cells (GPT-2~XL nucleus); generalization to other model--strategy configurations under prompted conditions is not established.
57	Detection features degrade under domain shift \cite{pudasaini2026_detection_fails}, and curated synthetic data may not trigger collapse \cite{falahati2026_curated}, attenuating the fingerprints we exploit.
58	
59	\section*{Ethics Statement}
60	
61	This work uses publicly available pretrained language models (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, GPT-2~XL) and Wikipedia text as seed data.
62	No human subjects were involved, no private or sensitive data were collected, and no new models were released.
63	Our method is intended to support data provenance and training-data auditing; we acknowledge that depth estimation tools could in principle be misused to circumvent content moderation, though such risks are shared with all detection-oriented research.
64	Code and data will be released upon acceptance.
65	This research used AI coding assistants for experiment implementation and writing drafts.
66	All scientific decisions, experimental design, and result interpretation were conducted by the authors.
```

</details>

seed=43 yields $+$0.1 to $+$3.2\\,pp higher accuracy, with 2/3 deltas statistically significant (non-overlapping CIs).\nFeature rankings and depth ordering are consistent across seeds, but single-seed absolute accuracy carries ${\\sim}$1--3\\,pp uncertainty; cross-cell comparisons should be interpreted cautiously.\n\n\\paragraph{Additional caveats.}\nDepth signal scales with passage length (68.4\\% at 128 tokens, 77.8\\% at 512; Appendix~\\ref{app:length_ablation}), saturating near 500 tokens.\nPrompted generation was validated on only 1 of 9 cells (GPT-2~XL nucleus); generalization to other model--strategy configurations under prompted conditions is not established.\nDetection features degrade under domain shift \\cite{pudasaini2026_detection_fails}, and curated synthetic data may not trigger collapse \\cite{falahati2026_curated}, attenuating the fingerprints we exploit.\n\n\\section*{Ethics Statement}\n\nThis work uses publicly available pretrained language models (Pythia-1.4B, OLMo-1B, GPT-2~XL) and Wikipedia text as seed data.\nNo human subjects were involved, no private or sensitive data were collected, and no new models were released.\nOur method is intended to support data provenance and training-data auditing; we acknowledge that depth estimation tools could in principle be misused to circumvent content moderation, though such risks are shared with all detection-oriented research.\nCode and data will be released upon acceptance.\nThis research used AI coding assistants for experiment implementation and writing drafts.\nAll scientific decisions, experimental design, and result interpretation were conducted by the authors.",

```text
1	\section{Generalization and Robustness}
2	\label{sec:generalization}
3	
4	We test whether atlas accuracy (68.9--85.9\%) reflects genuine statistical regularities across eight generalization axes (five below, three in Appendix).
5	
6	\subsection{Classifier Comparison and Surface-Text Ablation}
7	\label{sec:gen:baselines}
8	
9	Table~\ref{tab:baselines} compares ordinal depth estimation methods across all 9 atlas cells.
10	RF, XGBoost, and LightGBM on the same 19 features converge within 0.4\,pp per cell, confirming a feature-determined ceiling; on Pythia nucleus, CORAL \cite{coral2020} (78.1\%) and DivEye \cite{diveye2026} (78.2\%) match RF within 0.6\,pp ($p>0.05$).
11	
12	\paragraph{Binary detectors and membership inference.}
13	Four methods designed for binary AI-text detection or membership inference, when calibrated for ordinal depth, all fall 43--48\,pp below RF (Table~\ref{tab:baselines}).
14	Min-K\% Prob \cite{shi2024_minkprob} (33.2\%) outperforms DetectGPT \cite{mitchell2023_detectgpt} (30.7\%) by 2.5\,pp, consistent with token-level log-probabilities encoding more depth information than perturbation-based curvature; both remain near chance.
15	CORAL ordinal calibration does not close the gap (Min-K\% CORAL d1--d3 BalAcc: 40.4\% vs.\ RF 71.6\%), confirming that the bottleneck is input signal, not calibration.
16	Binary detector scores (Binoculars, Fast-DetectGPT) repurposed as RF features reach 74.2\% on a single cell but average only 30--35\% across 9 cells (Appendix~\ref{app:detector_baselines}).
17	Adding Min-K\% probability as a 20th RF feature yields +0.48\,pp (78.4\%$\to$78.9\%); Min-K\% ranks \#3 by importance (0.095), below \texttt{p90\_ppl} (0.161) and \texttt{var\_surprisal} (0.126), while DetectGPT contributes negligibly (\#17, 0.026).
18	The gain is consistent across 8 of 9 cells but small, confirming that the 19 statistical features already capture the dominant ordinal signal.
19	
20	\paragraph{Surface-text ablation.}
21	Single-feature regression on mean perplexity performs at chance (25.1\%); zero-shot LLMs (DeepSeek V3.2, 36.2\%; Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, 30.4\%) also fail (43\,pp below the best RF cell; Appendix~\ref{app:zeroshot_prompt}); depth signal resides in distributional statistics inaccessible from surface text.
22	
23	\begin{table}[t]
24	\centering
25	\small
26	\caption{Ordinal depth estimation: method comparison (mean across 9 atlas cells). Chance = 25\% (4-class) / 33.3\% (d1--d3). Min-K\%: $K{=}20$, CORAL calibration. DetectGPT: gpt2-large scoring, t5-large perturbation ($n{=}5$). 5-fold CV; SD $\leq$ 0.9\,pp for feature-based methods.}
27	\label{tab:baselines}
28	\begin{tabular}{@{}lcc@{}}
29	\toprule
30	Method & 4-Cl Acc (\%) & d1--d3 BalAcc (\%) \\
31	\midrule
32	RF 19-feature (ours) & \textbf{78.4} & \textbf{71.6} \\
33	\midrule
34	Zero-shot LLM (best) & 36.2 & --- \\
35	Min-K\% Prob (CORAL) & 33.2 & 40.4 \\
36	DetectGPT (perturbation) & 30.7 & 24.3 \\
37	Random & 25.0 & 33.3 \\
38	\bottomrule
39	\end{tabular}
40	\end{table}
41	
42	Convergence confirms the signal resides in the features (three classifiers match within 0.8\,pp under cross-model transfer; Appendix~\ref{app:learned_estimator}).
43	Perplexity features alone recover 66.0\% ($-$0.5\,pp vs.\ full), while repetition/diversity features alone fall 13.6\,pp below; perplexity degradation is the dominant depth marker.
44	Raw perplexity alone performs at chance (25.1\%); the strongest single feature (\texttt{p90\_ppl}) reaches 58.3\% ($-$8.3\,pp vs.\ full); multi-dimensional characterization is required. Figure~\ref{fig:confusion_matrix} shows d0 near-perfectly identified; errors concentrate at d2/d3 in-distribution and spread to d1--d3 under transfer (RF seed std $= 0.06$\,pp; Appendix~\ref{app:rf_seed}).
45	Cross-seed replication across all three model families confirms consistent feature rankings and depth ordering; absolute accuracy varies by $+$0.1 to $+$3.2\,pp (same direction), with 2/3 deltas statistically significant (Appendix~\ref{app:seed_replication}).
46	
47	\begin{figure}[t]
48	\centering
49	\includegraphics[width=\columnwidth]{figures/fig_confusion_matrix.pdf}
50	\caption{Confusion matrices for the best in-distribution setting (GPT-2 XL, temp $\tau{=}0.9$, 85.9\%) and the best cross-model leave-one-out transfer (OLMo holdout, 69.2\%). d0 recall exceeds 99\% in both; errors spread across the d1--d3 range under transfer.}
51	\label{fig:confusion_matrix}
52	\end{figure}
53	
54	\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer}
55	\label{sec:gen:crossmodel}
56	
57	We perform leave-one-model-family-out evaluation: train on two families (nucleus only), test on the held-out third (Table~\ref{tab:cross_model}).
58	
59	\begin{table}[t]
60	\centering
61	\small
62	\caption{Cross-model transfer (leave-one-out, nucleus only). Chance = 25\% / 33\% (d1--d3). d1--d3 bal.\ acc: Pythia 49.9\%, OLMo 59.2\%, GPT-2~XL 57.2\% (mean 55.4\%). Ordinal quality: MAE\,=\,0.39, QWK\,=\,0.81 (QWK$_{\text{d1--d3}}$\,=\,0.53); 94.8\% of errors at $|\Delta| \leq 1$.}
63	\label{tab:cross_model}
64	\begin{tabular}{lccccc}
65	\toprule
66	Holdout & Acc & d0 & d1 & d2 & d3 \\
67	 & (\%) & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. \\
68	\midrule
69	Pythia & 62.4 & 100.0 & 49.3 & 26.8 & 73.6 \\
70	OLMo & \textbf{69.2} & 99.3 & 75.5 & 43.3 & 58.8 \\
71	GPT-2 XL & 67.6 & 98.5 & 74.7 & 52.6 & 44.5 \\
72	\midrule
73	Mean & 66.4 & 99.3 & 66.5 & 40.9 & 59.0 \\
74	\bottomrule
75	\end{tabular}
76	\end{table}
77	
78	Mean transfer accuracy is 66.4\% (2.7$\times$ chance); d0 recall ($>$98\%) inflates the headline (d1--d3 balanced mean 55.4\%).
79	
80	\subsection{Cross-Strategy Transfer}
81	\label{sec:gen:crossstrategy}
82	
83	Table~\ref{tab:cross_strategy} reports leave-one-strategy-out transfer.
84	
85	\begin{table}[t]
86	\centering
87	\small
88	\caption{Cross-strategy transfer (leave-one-out). Chance = 25\% / 33\% (d1--d3). d0 recall $\geq$89\% inflates headline; d1--d3 mean: 45.9\%.}
89	\label{tab:cross_strategy}
90	\begin{tabular}{lccc}
91	\toprule
92	Holdout & Pythia & OLMo & GPT-2 XL \\
93	 & Acc (\%) & Acc (\%) & Acc (\%) \\
94	\midrule
95	nucleus & \textbf{69.4} & \textbf{65.4} & \textbf{59.9} \\
96	temp ($\tau{=}0.9$) & 62.8 & 58.2 & 56.1 \\
97	top-$k$ ($k{=}50$) & 51.5 & 54.9 & 53.6 \\
98	\bottomrule
99	\end{tabular}
100	\end{table}
101	
102	Mean cross-strategy accuracy (59.1\%) falls 7\,pp below cross-model transfer (66.4\%); d1--d3 accuracy drops to 45.9\% (chance = 33\%).
103	The core finding is structural: training on nucleus + top-$k$ inverts the d1/d2 boundary under temperature (d1v2 AUC $<$ 0.5), because temperature scaling accelerates convergence to a low-entropy attractor (\S\ref{sec:method:theory}); depth-$d$ tail statistics under temperature therefore resemble nucleus depth-$(d{+}1)$.
104	Strategy confusion concentrates between temperature and top-$k$ (31.2\%), consistent with their opposing effects on the perplexity tail.
105	As a proof-of-concept, a two-stage pipeline (strategy identification at 57.5\% + depth classification) recovers 11.5\,pp, with a strategy-invariant floor of 67.6\% (Limitations); end-to-end PLMs lag RF by 20\,pp (Appendix~\ref{app:e2e}).
106	Conditional decomposition reveals that the four-class accuracy gap between correct and wrong strategy routing is moderate ($+$4.6\,pp: 72.6\% vs.\ 68.0\%, $N{=}103{,}498$ / $76{,}502$) because d0 accuracy increases under misrouting (${\sim}$99.6\%); the d1--d3 balanced accuracy gap is 31.5\,pp (70.6\% vs.\ 39.1\%): cross-strategy difficulty is a feature-space misalignment at inter-depth boundaries, not uniform noise.
107	
108	\paragraph{Prompted generation.}
109	Replicating the GPT-2~XL nucleus pipeline with topic-seeded generation yields 69.5\% (95\% CI $[68.9, 70.2]$, $-$13.8\,pp vs.\ BOS-seeded, $2.8\times$ chance), with monotonic AUC decay and identical top features (Appendix~\ref{app:prompted_gen}): prompt conditioning attenuates but does not eliminate the depth signal.
110	
111	%% ----------------------------------------------------------------
112	\subsection{Preliminary Cross-Scale Observations}
113	\label{sec:gen:crossscale}
114	
115	Extending to 7--8B under QLoRA, Llama-3.1-8B Instruct \cite{llama3} achieves 70.2\% ($\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}4$) and Qwen2.5-7B \cite{qwen2.5} 60.1\% ($\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{>}5$).
116	At 14B (Qwen2.5-14B, self-reference model), accuracy drops to 54.1\% ($\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$; d0v1 AUC\,=\,0.643), though d1--d3 discrimination persists (balanced accuracy 57.4\%, chance 33\%); the depth signal attenuates with scale but does not vanish (Appendix~\ref{app:cross_scale_full}).
117	These results are confounded by simultaneous changes in quantization (4-bit QLoRA), sample size (500 vs.\ 5{,}000), decoding strategy (nucleus only), vocabulary size, and (for 14B) reference model; they should be treated as directional evidence rather than definitive conclusions.
118	A base-model control (Llama-3.1-8B, 59.9\%, non-monotonic AUC) vs.\ instruction-tuned (70.2\%, monotonic) suggests instruction tuning \emph{regularizes} recursive degradation; a 1.5B instruct control (47.4\%, $1.9\times$ chance) confirms at matched scale (Appendix~\ref{app:instruct_1p5b}; confounds in Appendix~\ref{app:cross_scale_confounds}).
119	Cross-model chains (84.75\%), cross-domain (arXiv 74.1\%), and LoRA rank sensitivity ($\Delta{=}4.65$\,pp; Appendix~\ref{app:cross_scale_full}) further confirm robustness.

Min-K\\% ranks \\#3 by importance (0.095), below \\texttt{p90\\_ppl} (0.161) and \\texttt{var\\_surprisal} (0.126), while DetectGPT contributes negligibly (\\#17, 0.026).\nThe gain is consistent across 8 of 9 cells but small, confirming that the 19 statistical features already capture the dominant ordinal signal.\n\n\\paragraph{Surface-text ablation.}\nSingle-feature regression on mean perplexity performs at chance (25.1\\%); zero-shot LLMs (DeepSeek V3.2, 36.2\\%; Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, 30.4\\%) also fail (43\\,pp below the best RF cell; Appendix~\\ref{app:zeroshot_prompt}); depth signal resides in distributional statistics inaccessible from surface text.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Ordinal depth estimation: method comparison (mean across 9 atlas cells). Chance = 25\\% (4-class) / 33.3\\% (d1--d3). Min-K\\%: $K{=}20$, CORAL calibration. DetectGPT: gpt2-large scoring, t5-large perturbation ($n{=}5$). 5-fold CV; SD $\\leq$ 0.9\\,pp for feature-based methods.}\n\\label{tab:baselines}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lcc@{}}\n\\toprule\nMethod & 4-Cl Acc (\\%) & d1--d3 BalAcc (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nRF 19-feature (ours) & \\textbf{78.4} & \\textbf{71.6} \\\\\n\\midrule\nZero-shot LLM (best) & 36.2 & --- \\\\\nMin-K\\% Prob (CORAL) & 33.2 & 40.4 \\\\\nDetectGPT (perturbation) & 30.7 & 24.3 \\\\\nRandom & 25.0 & 33.3 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nConvergence confirms the signal resides in the features (three classifiers match within 0.8\\,pp under cross-model transfer; Appendix~\\ref{app:learned_estimator}).\nPerplexity features alone recover 66.0\\% ($-$0.5\\,pp vs.\\ full), while repetition/diversity features alone fall 13.6\\,pp below; perplexity degradation is the dominant depth marker.\nRaw perplexity alone performs at chance (25.1\\%); the strongest single feature (\\texttt{p90\\_ppl}) reaches 58.3\\% ($-$8.3\\,pp vs.\\ full); multi-dimensional characterization is required. Figure~\\ref{fig:confusion_matrix} shows d0 near-perfectly identified; errors concentrate at d2/d3 in-distribution and spread to d1--d3 under transfer (RF seed std $= 0.06$\\,pp; Appendix~\\ref{app:rf_seed}).\nCross-seed replication across all three model families confirms consistent feature rankings and depth ordering; absolute accuracy varies by $+$0.1 to $+$3.2\\,pp (same direction), with 2/3 deltas statistically significant (Appendix~\\ref{app:seed_replication}).\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=\\columnwidth]{figures/fig_confusion_matrix.pdf}\n\\caption{Confusion matrices for the best in-distribution setting (GPT-2 XL, temp $\\tau{=}0.9$, 85.9\\%) and the best cross-model leave-one-out transfer (OLMo holdout, 69.2\\%). d0 recall exceeds 99\\% in both; errors spread across the d1--d3 range under transfer.}\n\\label{fig:confusion_matrix}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Model Transfer}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossmodel}\n\nWe perform leave-one-model-family-out evaluation: train on two families (nucleus only), test on the held-out third (Table~\\ref{tab:cross_model}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-model transfer (leave-one-out, nucleus only). Chance = 25\\% / 33\\% (d1--d3). d1--d3 bal.\\ acc: Pythia 49.9\\%, OLMo 59.2\\%, GPT-2~XL 57.2\\% (mean 55.4\\%). Ordinal quality: MAE\\,=\\,0.39, QWK\\,=\\,0.81 (QWK$_{\\text{d1--d3}}$\\,=\\,0.53); 94.8\\% of errors at $|\\Delta| \\leq 1$.}\n\\label{tab:cross_model}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\nHoldout & Acc & d0 & d1 & d2 & d3 \\\\\n & (\\%) & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. & Rec. \\\\\n\\midrule\nPythia & 62.4 & 100.0 & 49.3 & 26.8 & 73.6 \\\\\nOLMo & \\textbf{69.2} & 99.3 & 75.5 & 43.3 & 58.8 \\\\\nGPT-2 XL & 67.6 & 98.5 & 74.7 & 52.6 & 44.5 \\\\\n\\midrule\nMean & 66.4 & 99.3 & 66.5 & 40.9 & 59.0 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nMean transfer accuracy is 66.4\\% (2.7$\\times$ chance); d0 recall ($>$98\\%) inflates the headline (d1--d3 balanced mean 55.4\\%).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Strategy Transfer}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossstrategy}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:cross_strategy} reports leave-one-strategy-out transfer.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-strategy transfer (leave-one-out). Chance = 25\\% / 33\\% (d1--d3). d0 recall $\\geq$89\\% inflates headline; d1--d3 mean: 45.9\\%.}\n\\label{tab:cross_strategy}\n\\begin{tabular}{lccc}\n\\toprule\nHoldout & Pythia & OLMo & GPT-2 XL \\\\\n & Acc (\\%) & Acc (\\%) & Acc (\\%) \\\\\n\\midrule\nnucleus & \\textbf{69.4} & \\textbf{65.4} & \\textbf{59.9} \\\\\ntemp ($\\tau{=}0.9$) & 62.8 & 58.2 & 56.1 \\\\\ntop-$k$ ($k{=}50$) & 51.5 & 54.9 & 53.6 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\nMean cross-strategy accuracy (59.1\\%) falls 7\\,pp below cross-model transfer (66.4\\%); d1--d3 accuracy drops to 45.9\\% (chance = 33\\%).\nThe core finding is structural: training on nucleus + top-$k$ inverts the d1/d2 boundary under temperature (d1v2 AUC $<$ 0.5), because temperature scaling accelerates convergence to a low-entropy attractor (\\S\\ref{sec:method:theory}); depth-$d$ tail statistics under temperature therefore resemble nucleus depth-$(d{+}1)$.\nStrategy confusion concentrates between temperature and top-$k$ (31.2\\%), consistent with their opposing effects on the perplexity tail.\nAs a proof-of-concept, a two-stage pipeline (strategy identification at 57.5\\% + depth classification) recovers 11.5\\,pp, with a strategy-invariant floor of 67.6\\% (Limitations); end-to-end PLMs lag RF by 20\\,pp (Appendix~\\ref{app:e2e}).\nConditional decomposition reveals that the four-class accuracy gap between correct and wrong strategy routing is moderate ($+$4.6\\,pp: 72.6\\% vs.\\ 68.0\\%, $N{=}103{,}498$ / $76{,}502$) because d0 accuracy increases under misrouting (${\\sim}$99.6\\%); the d1--d3 balanced accuracy gap is 31.5\\,pp (70.6\\% vs.\\ 39.1\\%): cross-strategy difficulty is a feature-space misalignment at inter-depth boundaries, not uniform noise.\n\n\\paragraph{Prompted generation.}\nReplicating the GPT-2~XL nucleus pipeline with topic-seeded generation yields 69.5\\% (95\\% CI $[68.9, 70.2]$, $-$13.8\\,pp vs.\\ BOS-seeded, $2.8\\times$ chance), with monotonic AUC decay and identical top features (Appendix~\\ref{app:prompted_gen}): prompt conditioning attenuates but does not eliminate the depth signal.\n\n%% ----------------------------------------------------------------\n\\subsection{Preliminary Cross-Scale Observations}\n\\label{sec:gen:crossscale}\n\nExtending to 7--8B under QLoRA, Llama-3.1-8B Instruct \\cite{llama3} achieves 70.2\\% ($\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}4$) and Qwen2.5-7B \\cite{qwen2.5} 60.1\\% ($\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{>}5$).\nAt 14B (Qwen2.5-14B, self-reference model), accuracy drops to 54.1\\% ($\\mathrm{EDD}_{0.70}{=}0$; d0v1 AUC\\,=\\,0.643), though d1--d3 discrimination persists (balanced accuracy 57.4\\%, chance 33\\%); the depth signal attenuates with scale but does not vanish (Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_scale_full}).\nThese results are confounded by simultaneous changes in quantization (4-bit QLoRA), sample size (500 vs.\\ 5{,}000), decoding strategy (nucleus only), vocabulary size, and (for 14B) reference model; they should be treated as directional evidence rather than definitive conclusions.\nA base-model control (Llama-3.1-8B, 59.9\\%, non-monotonic AUC) vs.\\ instruction-tuned (70.2\\%, monotonic) suggests instruction tuning \\emph{regularizes} recursive degradation; a 1.5B instruct control (47.4\\%, $1.9\\times$ chance) confirms at matched scale (Appendix~\\ref{app:instruct_1p5b}; confounds in Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_scale_confounds}).\nCross-model chains (84.75\\%), cross-domain (arXiv 74.1\\%), and LoRA rank sensitivity ($\\Delta{=}4.65$\\,pp; Appendix~\\ref{app:cross_scale_full}) further confirm robustness.",

    }

```text
1	\section{Discussion and Conclusion}
2	\label{sec:discussion}
3	
4	This paper formalizes recursive generation depth estimation as ordinal classification and provides both theoretical grounding (\S\ref{sec:method:theory}) and empirical characterization across three 1--1.5B base model families and three decoding strategies in controlled settings.
5	The tail erosion mechanism and strategy-dependent convergence rates predicted in \S\ref{sec:method:theory} are confirmed across the atlas; five features suffice for 97.6\% of full performance, consistent with the low effective dimensionality of the degradation process.
6	Extending all three families to d4 yields 71--80\% five-class accuracy (chance = 20\%; Appendix~\ref{app:depth4_ext}); aggregate statistics (mean perplexity) saturate before tail statistics at this depth, and this saturation provides a physical explanation for the EDD ceiling.
7	
8	Cross-strategy transfer is the primary bottleneck (d1--d3: 45.9\%, chance = 33\%), with d1/d2 boundary inversion under strategy mismatch revealing that different decoding strategies induce qualitatively distinct degradation trajectories; a proof-of-concept two-stage pipeline (strategy identification + depth classification) recovers 11.5\,pp.
9	For data curation, RF filtering (F1\,=\,0.997) outperforms perplexity thresholds (F1\,=\,0.845) for depth identification, but downstream training improvement is non-significant ($p > 0.4$; Appendix~\ref{app:curation_poc}, \ref{app:downstream}).
10	In principle, depth estimation could complement C2PA \cite{c2pa2024} and watermarking \cite{kirchenbauer2023_watermark} by providing a retroactive, cooperation-free signal; cross-model chains (84.75\%) confirm the signal is process-level, though deployment beyond controlled settings requires addressing cross-strategy transfer.
11	The weakest link is d2 recall (26.8\% under cross-model transfer); BOS-seeded generation was validated under prompted conditions for only 1/9 cells ($-$13.8\,pp; \S\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}).
12	Cross-strategy transfer and depth-quality entanglement remain open challenges (Limitations; Appendix~\ref{app:extended_discussion}).
13	
```

</details>

```text
1	\section{LoRA Fine-Tuning Protocol}
2	\label{app:lora_protocol}
3	
4	Table~\ref{tab:lora_protocol} lists the full LoRA fine-tuning configuration used for recursive chain construction (\S\ref{sec:method:benchmark}).
5	At each depth $k$, a fresh LoRA adapter is initialized on the base model and trained on $\mathcal{D}_{k-1}$; adapters are not inherited across depths.
6	The HuggingFace Trainer default linear decay schedule is used with no warmup.
7	Target modules are model-specific: \texttt{query\_key\_value} (Pythia), \texttt{att\_proj} (OLMo), and \texttt{c\_attn} (GPT-2~XL).
8	Cross-scale experiments (\S\ref{sec:gen:crossscale}) use QLoRA (4-bit NF4) with $r{=}16$, $\alpha{=}32$.
9	
10	\begin{table}[h]
11	\centering
12	\small
13	\caption{LoRA fine-tuning hyperparameters for the primary 1--1.5B benchmark. Identical across all three model families and all depth levels.}
14	\label{tab:lora_protocol}
15	\begin{tabular}{@{}ll@{}}
16	\toprule
17	Parameter & Value \\
18	\midrule
19	LoRA rank $r$ & 16 \\
20	LoRA alpha $\alpha$ & 32 \\
21	LoRA dropout & 0.05 \\
22	Bias & none \\
23	Epochs per depth & 3 \\
24	Learning rate & $2 \times 10^{-4}$ \\
25	LR schedule & linear decay (no warmup) \\
26	Batch size (effective) & 16 ($8 \times 2$ grad.\ accum.) \\
27	Max sequence length & 512 tokens \\
28	Precision & bfloat16 \\
29	Adapter init & from scratch (base model) \\
30	Seed & 42 (primary), 43 (replication) \\
31	\bottomrule
32	\end{tabular}
33	\end{table}
34	
35	\section{Additional Results}
36	\label{app:additional}
37	
38	\subsection{Top-$k$ Feature Trajectories}
39	\label{app:topk_trajectories}
40	
41	Figure~\ref{fig:feature_trajectory} in the main text shows feature trajectories for nucleus ($p{=}0.95$) and temperature ($\tau{=}0.9$) sampling.
42	Top-$k$ ($k{=}50$) trajectories follow the same monotonically decreasing trend for all features but with compressed inter-depth spacing, consistent with the signal attenuation effect discussed in \S\ref{sec:atlas:strategy}.
43	Concretely, the d0$\to$d1 drop in \texttt{p90\_ppl} under top-$k$ is 78\% (vs.\ 87\% for nucleus), and the d2$\to$d3 reduction is 31\% (vs.\ 39\% for nucleus).
44	Despite the magnitude reduction, feature rank ordering across depths is preserved, which explains why ordinal classifiers maintain directionality even under top-$k$ conditions.
45	
46	\subsection{Permutation Importance}
47	\label{app:permutation}
48	
49	To verify that the Gini-based importance rankings in \S\ref{sec:atlas:features} are not an artifact of the impurity measure, we compute permutation importance (5-fold CV, 10 repeats) for all 9 atlas cells.
50	The top-2 features are stable across measures: \texttt{p90\_ppl} ranks in the top 2 in 9/9 cells under both Gini and permutation importance, and \texttt{var\_surprisal} in 8/9 (vs.\ 7/9 for Gini, with the additional cell being OLMo top-$k$; the single SHAP exception is GPT-2~XL top-$k$, where \texttt{p75\_ppl} displaces \texttt{var\_surprisal} to rank 3).
51	Kendall's $W{=}0.90$ across all three methods (Gini, permutation, SHAP) indicates strong concordance ($p{<}0.005$).
52	Pairwise Spearman correlations are: SHAP--Gini $\rho{=}0.93$, SHAP--permutation $\rho{=}0.84$, Gini--permutation $\rho{=}0.76$.
53	Per-cell top-5 overlap between SHAP and permutation importance is 3--4 out of 5 features; the aggregated (mean-across-cells) top-5 sets are identical; the top features are therefore robust to the choice of importance measure at the aggregate level, though per-cell rankings exhibit model-dependent variation.
54	
55	\subsection{Per-Class Recall}
56	\label{app:per_class}
57	
58	Table~\ref{tab:per_class_recall} reports per-class recall across all 9 atlas cells (4-class in-distribution, 5-fold CV).
59	Depth~0 recall exceeds 97\% uniformly.
60	Among generated depths, d2 is the hardest class in every cell; the deficit is most pronounced under top-$k$ sampling (46--53\%), where vocabulary truncation removes the tail tokens that carry depth-discriminative signal.
61	
62	\begin{table}[h]
63	\centering
64	\small
65	\caption{Per-class recall (\%) for all 9 atlas cells. d2 is the lowest-recall class in every cell. Boldface marks the highest d2 recall.}
66	\label{tab:per_class_recall}
67	\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccc@{}}
68	\toprule
69	Cell & d0 & d1 & d2 & d3 \\
70	\midrule
71	Pythia, nuc.       & 99.3 & 81.9 & 59.2 & 74.4 \\
72	Pythia, $\tau$     & 99.2 & 79.9 & 56.0 & 72.1 \\
73	Pythia, top-$k$    & 98.7 & 71.6 & 44.2 & 60.9 \\
74	OLMo, nuc.         & 97.8 & 82.1 & 60.6 & 75.3 \\
75	OLMo, $\tau$       & 99.3 & 87.1 & 65.6 & 77.1 \\
76	OLMo, top-$k$      & 99.2 & 80.9 & 50.4 & 67.2 \\
77	GPT-2~XL, nuc.     & 98.5 & 87.4 & 72.8 & 78.5 \\
78	GPT-2~XL, $\tau$   & 99.0 & 91.1 & \textbf{73.0} & 80.5 \\
79	GPT-2~XL, top-$k$  & 98.7 & 81.3 & 54.8 & 68.4 \\
80	\bottomrule
81	\end{tabular}
82	\end{table}
83	
84	\subsection{DCOL Iteration Details}
85	\label{app:dcol_iterations}
86	
87	Table~\ref{tab:dcol_iterations} summarizes all six DCOL iterations.
88	DeBERTa-v3-large exhibited systematic numerical instability across five configurations; RoBERTa-large resolved stability but could not close the accuracy gap with feature-based methods.
89	
90	\begin{table}[h]
91	\centering
92	\small
93	\caption{DCOL iteration summary. All experiments use Pythia-1.4B nucleus data. RF baseline: 78.7\%.}
94	\label{tab:dcol_iterations}
95	\begin{tabular}{@{}llp{2.2cm}p{2.8cm}@{}}
96	\toprule
97	Ver. & Backbone & Config & Outcome \\
98	\midrule
99	v1 & DeBERTa & FT, m\_lr $10^{-3}$ & Embed.\ collapse (31.5\%) \\
100	v2 & DeBERTa & FT, m\_lr $10^{-4}$ & OOM \\
```

108	
109	\subsection{Zero-Shot LLM Prompt Template}
110	\label{app:zeroshot_prompt}
111	
112	Both DeepSeek V3.2 and Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct use identical prompting.
113	The system message defines recursive generation depth with per-depth descriptions (depth~0: human-written; depth~1--3: successive LLM generation cycles) and lists expected textual patterns at each depth (vocabulary richness, repetition, coherence).
114	The user message presents the passage (truncated to ${\approx}$384 words) and requests a JSON response containing an integer depth prediction (0--3), a confidence score, and a one-sentence reasoning.
115	No in-context examples are provided.
116	
117	\begin{quote}
118	\small
119	\textbf{System:} ``You are an expert at detecting AI-generated text and estimating its generation depth. `Recursive generation depth' refers to how many times a text has been through an LLM generation cycle: Depth~0: Original human-written text [\ldots] Depth~3: Text generated by an LLM given depth-2 text as input. Key patterns by depth: Depth~0 (human): Rich vocabulary [\ldots] Depth~3 (third-gen): Noticeable repetition, formulaic structure [\ldots]''
120	
121	\textbf{User:} ``Analyze the following text and estimate its recursive generation depth (0, 1, 2, or 3). TEXT: \{text\}. Respond with ONLY a JSON object: \{\texttt{"depth"}: $\langle$int 0--3$\rangle$, \texttt{"confidence"}: $\langle$float 0--1$\rangle$, \texttt{"reasoning"}: $\langle$str$\rangle$\}''
122	\end{quote}
123	
124	\subsection{Temperature Robustness}
125	\label{app:multi_tau}
126	
127	The cross-strategy results in \S\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy} use a single temperature ($\tau{=}0.9$).
128	To test whether depth signal generalizes across temperatures, we repeat the classification pipeline at $\tau \in \{0.5, 0.7, 0.9, 1.1\}$ on Pythia-1.4B.
129	
130	\paragraph{Within-$\tau$ robustness.}
131	For $\tau \in [0.5, 0.9]$, d1-vs-d2 AUC exceeds 0.80 (peak 0.851 at $\tau{=}0.7$, comparable to nucleus $p{=}0.95$'s 0.861) and 4-class accuracy ranges from 75.4\% to 77.3\% (Table~\ref{tab:multi_tau_within}); the depth signal is therefore not an artifact of a single temperature.
132	The same top-5 features (\texttt{var\_surprisal}, \texttt{p90\_ppl}, \texttt{mean\_ppl}, \texttt{mean\_surprisal}, \texttt{p75\_ppl}) dominate across all $\tau$ values with only minor rank-order variation.
133	
134	\paragraph{Collapse at $\tau{=}1.1$: memorization as prerequisite.}
135	At $\tau{=}1.1$, 4-class accuracy drops to 57.2\% and d1v2 AUC falls to 0.528.
136	Training loss reveals the mechanism: at low temperature ($\tau{=}0.5$), fine-tuning loss decreases steeply across recursive depths ($2.27 \to 0.42 \to 0.16$), a sign of rapid memorization of the model's own output.
137	At $\tau{=}1.1$, loss remains nearly flat ($2.27 \to 1.91 \to 1.85$); high-entropy output resists memorization, so depth-dependent distributional shifts do not accumulate.
138	This establishes \emph{memorization as a prerequisite} for detectable depth signal: recursive depth is discriminable only when successive fine-tuning iterations progressively reshape the output distribution.
139	
140	\paragraph{Cross-$\tau$ non-transferability.}
141	Leave-one-$\tau$-out transfer yields mean accuracy of 46.7\% with mean d1v2 AUC of 0.319 (Table~\ref{tab:multi_tau_cross}), worse than cross-strategy transfer (59.1\%; \S\ref{sec:gen:crossstrategy}).
142	Holdout $\tau{=}0.5$ produces inverted d1v2 AUC (0.176); different temperatures therefore produce surprisal distributions with incompatible shapes.
143	This extends the strategy-specificity finding: even within a single decoding family, the feature space is temperature-specific; strategy-conditioned classifiers are therefore necessary.
144	
145	\paragraph{Ecological validity.}
146	On WikiText-103 (546 editorial passages), all three model-specific RF classifiers achieve $\geq$98.5\% d0 accuracy despite $+$621\% domain shift in mean perplexity, partly an easy case, as WikiText's higher perplexity places it farther from d1--d3 in feature space.
147	On noisier web-crawl data (5{,}000 C4 passages), FPR ranges from 4.7\% (OLMo) to 22.4\% (GPT-2~XL), concentrated in template/SEO content with high n-gram repetition (Table~\ref{tab:calibration}); deployment on heterogeneous web data requires model-specific calibration.
148

185	\subsection{Feature Overlap Analysis}
186	\label{app:feature_overlap}
187	
188	We probe whether end-to-end PLM representations capture the same depth signals as our statistical features via linear probing of [CLS] representations from DeBERTa-v3-base and RoBERTa-base.
189	PLM representations excel at surface lexical features (TTR: $R^2{=}0.85$, repeated bigrams) but fail to capture perplexity distribution statistics; \texttt{var\_surprisal} and \texttt{p90\_ppl} yield negative $R^2$ under linear probing; these features are not linearly accessible in the pretrained representation space.
190	This confirms that the 20pp accuracy gap between feature-based and end-to-end methods (\S\ref{app:e2e}) stems from complementary, not redundant, representations: PLMs capture lexical patterns while the targeted statistical features capture distributional tail properties that constitute the dominant depth markers.
191	
192	\subsection{End-to-End Learning}
193	\label{app:e2e}
194	
195	DeBERTa-v3-base \cite{he2021_deberta_v3} and RoBERTa-base \cite{liu2019_roberta} finetuned end-to-end achieve 58.3\% ($\pm$4.7\%) and 59.5\% ($\pm$3.2\%) respectively, lagging the RF baseline (78.7\%) by 20.4pp and 19.2pp.
196	The 1.2pp inter-backbone gap rules out architecture bias; Table~\ref{tab:e2e_detail} shows both backbones share the same failure mode (high d0 F1, $>$0.94, but collapsed d1/d2 performance, F1 $\leq$ 0.42), while RF achieves 0.596 d2 recall, a $>$2$\times$ advantage at the hardest depth class.
197

695:Binoculars~\cite{binoculars2024} and Fast-DetectGPT~\cite{fast_detectgpt2024} scores are computed on the Pythia-1.4B nucleus ($p{=}0.95$) subset and fed to: (a)~a log-likelihood rank classifier (Log-Rank, 64.0\%), (b)~a combined RF trained on both detector scores (Combined, 74.2\%), and (c)~the standard 19-feature RF (78.7\%).
697:Note that Fast-DetectGPT exhibits numerical overflow on Pythia nucleus d1 (mean perturbation score $>$28{,}000); results for this cell should be interpreted cautiously.
698:Across all 9 atlas cells, Binoculars and Fast-DetectGPT ordinal accuracy averages ${\sim}$35\% and ${\sim}$32\%, respectively.
701:DetectGPT~\cite{mitchell2023_detectgpt} (gpt2-large scoring model, t5-large perturbation model, 5 perturbations) achieves 30.7\% mean 4-class accuracy (d1--d3 BalAcc: 24.3\%).
702:Min-K\% Prob~\cite{shi2024_minkprob} ($K{=}20$) with CORAL ordinal calibration reaches 33.2\% (d1--d3 BalAcc: 40.4\%); the best single cell is gpt2xl\_temp09 at 50.6\%.
703:Min-K\%'s advantage over DetectGPT (+2.5\,pp) is consistent with token-level log-probabilities containing more depth-relevant information than perturbation-based curvature, but both remain within 10\,pp of chance.
707:\paragraph{Augmented feature set.}
708:To test whether detection scores carry complementary ordinal signal, we add Min-K\% probability ($K{=}20$) and DetectGPT perturbation discrepancy as additional RF features.
709:The 20-feature model (19 + Min-K\%) achieves 78.90\% mean 4-class accuracy (+0.48\,pp) and 72.20\% d1--d3 balanced accuracy (+0.60\,pp).
710:Min-K\% ranks \#3 by feature importance (0.095), behind \texttt{p90\_ppl} (0.161) and \texttt{var\_surprisal} (0.126); DetectGPT ranks \#17 (0.026).
711:The marginal improvement is consistent across 8 of 9 atlas cells, indicating that Min-K\%'s token-level log-probability signal provides weak but real complementary information to the passage-level distributional features.
712:The top-2 features (\texttt{p90\_ppl} + \texttt{var\_surprisal}) retain combined importance of 28.7\%, confirming that perplexity tail statistics remain the dominant depth markers even after augmentation.

/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:759:Watermarking \cite{kirchenbauer2023_watermark} provides high-confidence attribution when the generator cooperates but is inapplicable to unwatermarked text.

the best single cell is gpt2xl\\_temp09 at 50.6\\%.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:759:Watermarking \\cite{kirchenbauer2023_watermark} provides high-confidence attribution when the generator cooperates but is inapplicable to unwatermarked text.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/introduction.tex:9:Binary detectors such as Binoculars \\cite{binoculars2024}, Fast-DetectGPT \\cite{fast_detectgpt2024}, and DivEye \\cite{diveye2026} classify text as human-written or machine-generated, collapsing all depths $d \\geq 1$ into a single category.\n/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/introduction.tex:10:Watermarking \\cite{kirchenbauer2023_watermark} requires proactive embedding and cannot be applied retroactively.",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Xe2nb4WBGkK3mmL1GxejYu`</summary>

```text
{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — recursive_gen_depth_est\\n\\n## Target Venue\\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR May 25, 2026)\\n- Backup: AAAI 2027 (abstract July 25, full paper August 1, 2026)\\n- EMNLP 2026 special theme \\\"New Missions for NLP Research\\\" explicitly calls for work on data contamination, synthetic data feedback loops, and data governance — direct venue fit\\n\\n## Core Claims (3 条 + Discussion implication)\\n\\n1. **Ordinal depth estimation is feasible, accurate, and robust across scales and generation modes**: Given text alone, recursive generation depth (d ∈ {0,1,2,...,K}) can be estimated with high accuracy using statistical features from reference-model perplexity distributions. On our 3-model × 3-strategy benchmark (1–1.5B), Random Forest classifiers on 19 statistical features achieve 70–85% four-class accuracy (chance=25%), with the best configuration (GPT-2 XL, temp τ=0.9) reaching 85.4%. Feature-based methods (RF, CORAL, DivEye) consistently outperform single-feature baselines across all settings. **Scale robustness**: Clean FP16 13B experiment (Qwen2.5-14B, no quantization) confirms depth signal persists at mainstream model scale, addressing the \\\"small model only\\\" concern from prior 7B QLoRA experiments. **Ecological validity**: Prompted generation experiment (topic-seeded text, not BOS-only unconditional) confirms depth signal is not an artifact of unnatural generation mode.\\n\\n2. **Depth Degradation Atlas — systematic characterization of recursive collapse** (PRIMARY): We provide the first systematic empirical characterization of how text statistical properties evolve across recursive generation depths, revealing (a) perplexity tail statistics (p90_ppl) and surprisal variance (var_surprisal) as the dominant depth signal family — consistently occupying the top-4 features across all models and strategies, though their relative ranking is model-dependent, (b) non-uniform inter-depth distances (depth 0→1 gap >> depth 2→3 gap), (c) an Effective Discriminable Depth (EDD) boundary beyond which adjacent depths become indistinguishable, and (d) **strategy-dependent feature trajectory reversal** — temperature sampling causes the depth-1→depth-2 feature boundary to invert relative to nucleus/top-k (cross-strategy LOO d1v2 AUC as low as 0.13), demonstrating that decoding strategy fundamentally shapes the degradation trajectory rather than merely attenuating it, and (e) **accelerated convergence mechanism** — root cause analysis confirms a genuine structural distributional shift: temperature's uniform logit scaling (vs nucleus/top-k truncation) accelerates convergence to the attractor distribution, so temp09 depth N features ≈ nucleus/topk50 depth N+1 (GPT-2 XL temp09 d1 p90_ppl median=92.2, falls into nucleus/topk50 d2 range 65–92). This is not a simple scale artifact fixable by normalization — z-score normalization improves overall LR cross-strategy accuracy (25%→61%) but does **not** recover pairwise d1v2 AUC (GPT-2 XL 0.50→0.24, OLMo 0.50→0.19), and does **not** improve tree-based classifiers (RF Δ<0.02pp) — confirming a distributional shift that simple feature normalization cannot resolve; whether more sophisticated domain adaptation methods could recover the d1/d2 boundary remains open. This atlas spans 3 model families × 3 decoding strategies, with scale consistency confirmed at 14B.\\n\\n3. **Cross-strategy depth estimation via two-stage pipeline + strategy-invariant signal discovery**: Naive cross-strategy transfer degrades to 59.1% with d1v2 boundary inversion. We propose a two-stage solution: (a) strategy identification classifier (57.8% — strategies share more feature space than expected), then (b) strategy-conditioned depth classifiers. The pipeline improves cross-strategy accuracy by **+11.5pp** on average (59.1% → 70.5%), with per-model results: GPT-2 XL 73.0%, OLMo 69.4%, Pythia 69.3%. Oracle ceiling at 78.3%. **Caveat**: improvement is consistent across models but absolute accuracy varies (2/3 models individually below 70%); paper must claim \\\"+11.5pp improvement\\\" rather than \\\"≥70% universal recovery\\\". The surprising finding: even with imperfect strategy routing, the pipeline works because depth signal has a **strategy-invariant core** (~60% accuracy floor) plus a strategy-specific component. This reframes cross-strategy transfer from \\\"broken\\\" to \\\"substantially improved with clear ceiling for future work\\\".\\n\\n4. **[Discussion — Practical Implication]** Depth-graduated curation as alternative to binary filtering: predicted depth provides a finer-grained quality proxy than binary real/synthetic labels, enabling graduated weighting strategies. Conceptual argument supported by depth-accuracy correlation in 3×3 matrix data; full experimental validation deferred to future work (D012).\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: Web-scraped training corpora increasingly contain multi-generational synthetic content from uncontrolled model→data→model feedback loops. Existing detectors answer \\\"is this synthetic?\\\" but not \\\"how many recursive cycles has this undergone?\\\" — a critical distinction since 1st-gen synthetic data can improve training while deeply recursive text drives distribution collapse.\\n\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: Binary detectors (LOKI, DivEye, Binoculars) classify real vs. synthetic but discard ordinal depth information. EditLens estimates single-pass AI editing extent but not recursive depth. Model collapse studies (Drayson et al. EMNLP 2025; Shumailov et al. Nature 2024) characterize degradation patterns but assume depth is known a priori — no method estimates depth from text alone.\\n\\n- **Our framing**: We formalize ordinal generation depth estimation as a new task, construct a multi-generational benchmark spanning 1B–14B models across 3 decoding strategies and 4 depths, and provide the first **Depth Degradation Atlas** — a systematic empirical map of how text statistical properties evolve across recursive depths, models, and strategies. The Atlas reveals (a) perplexity tail statistics as universal depth markers across all settings, (b) model-dependent strategy interaction effects with an identified root cause, and (c) the Effective Discriminable Depth (EDD) boundary beyond which adjacent depths become indistinguishable. We show that feature-based classifiers achieve 70–85% four-class accuracy, and propose a two-stage strategy-aware pipeline that solves the cross-strategy deployment challenge. The depth signal persists under prompted generation (not just BOS-seeded), confirming ecological relevance.\\n\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\"On our multi-generational benchmark spanning model scales from 1B to 14B and 3 decoding strategies, feature-based ordinal depth estimation achieves 70–85% accuracy (chance=25%), with perplexity tail statistics (p90_ppl, var_surprisal) consistently among the top-4 features across all settings. The depth signal persists under prompted (topic-seeded) generation and at 14B scale without quantization. The Depth Degradation Atlas reveals that effective discriminable depth extends to depth 3 in 7/9 settings (T=0.75). We identify strategy-dependent feature trajectory reversal and propose a two-stage pipeline that restores near within-strategy accuracy for cross-strategy deployment.\\\"\\n- **Note**: 4-class (depth 0-3) is the confirmed setting. 6-class (depth 0-5) completed via plan_011.\\n\\n## DCOL Status — Contingency Activated (D010/D011, 2026-05-15)\\n\\nDCOL v1–v5 全部失败（embedding collapse / OOM / catastrophic forgetting / 超参违规 / NaN 梯度爆炸）。\\n\\n- **Claim 2 (Atlas/EDD) 升为 PRIMARY contribution**\\n- **Claim 1**: Feature-based methods (RF/CORAL/DivEye) 作为 depth estimation 方法，不依赖 DCOL\\n- **DCOL v6 (RoBERTa-large)**: 非阻塞尝试。成功 → Section 5 bonus；失败 → negative analysis section（\\\"contrastive ordinal learning 在文本深度估计的固有局限\\\"）\\n- **Paper type**: Task formalization + systematic empirical study + practical framework（非 method paper）\\n- Atlas + EDD + feature universality + model-strategy interaction = 充分支撑 EMNLP \\\"New Missions\\\" track\\n\\n## Venue Fit Risks\\n\\n- **\\\"Just an empirical study\\\" risk**: Primary risk now that DCOL is demoted. Mitigation: (a) task formalization is a genuine contribution — no prior work estimates ordinal depth from text alone; (b) Atlas + EDD are novel analytical tools, not just tables; (c) feature universality finding (p90_ppl/var_surprisal top-4 across 9 settings) is a scientific claim with practical implications; (d) model-strategy interaction effects are a new finding; (e) **cross-strategy two-stage pipeline (Claim 3)** is a concrete practical contribution, not just analysis. EMNLP \\\"New Missions\\\" track explicitly values problem formalization.\\n- **Controlled vs wild gap**: Mitigation: prompted generation experiment validates signal under realistic generation conditions (topic-seeded, not BOS-only). Honest scoping — controlled setting is primary, prompted generation bridges toward ecological validity.\\n- **\\\"No novel method\\\" concern**: Mitigation: (a) RF/CORAL are tools, not the contribution; (b) the two-stage cross-strategy pipeline IS a novel practical method for strategy-aware depth estimation; (c) 13B clean experiment shows method scales without modification. Analogous to benchmark/analysis papers at top venues (e.g., HELM, MMLU).\\n- **\\\"Small model only\\\" concern**: Eliminated by clean 13B FP16 experiment. Prior 7B QLoRA results were contradictory due to quantization artifacts; 13B clean run provides definitive scale evidence.\\n\\n## Dream-Derived Requirements (D002, 2026-05-14)\\n\\n三档(best 3.0 / median 3.0 / worst 2.5)一致指出的结构性必修项：\\n\\n### 论文写作硬约束\\n1. **DCOL loss 描述** — 若 v6 成功：Eq.1 必须包含 attractive + repulsive 双项（N-pair formulation）。若 v6 失败：negative analysis section 描述失败模式和根因分析，无需完整 loss inference 完整规格** — k 值、distance metric、sensitivity analysis\\n\\n### Framing 方向\\n8. **Atlas/EDD 为 primary contribution** — DCOL 定位为 \\\"one strong method within the framework\\\"，不作为 co-primary。三档审稿均认为 DCOL 是 incremental adaptation of CLOC。与 DCOL Contingency 一致。\\n9. **\\\"First systematic empirical map\\\" 需限定** — Shumailov/Drayson 已有 characterization, 我们的新颖性在 ordinal estimation lens + EDD formalization + multi-model multi-strategy coverage\\n\\n### Cross-Strategy 反转硬约束 (D015, Reviewer 降级条件)\\n10. **三档策略分类** — 论文不得将 nucleus 和 temperature 归为同一类 \\\"soft sampling\\\"。必须采用三档：nucleus（baseline 稳定）、temperature（信号增强但存在 d1v2 方向反转风险）、topk（信号衰减但方向一致）。D004 的 \\\"soft sampling\\\" 分组已被反转数据推翻。\\n11. **Discussion 必须含 per-strategy d1v2 AUC 表** — 包含 0.13/0.25 反转值 + **已确认的机制**：temperature 均匀缩放 logit（非截断）加速模型收敛到 attractor 分布（genuine structural distributional shift），temp09 depth N ≈ nucleus/topk50 depth N+1。z-score 标准化提升整体 LR 跨策略 acc（25%→61%）但**不恢复** pairwise d1v2 AUC（GPT-2 XL 0.50→0.24, OLMo 0.50→0.19），证实这是结构性几何偏移而非可校正的尺度差异。\\n12. **Limitations 必须写明跨策略部署约束** — \\\"跨策略部署时需先验知道目标策略类型，否则 d1/d2 预测可能反转。\\\"不可仅将反转作为正面发现消化。\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns\\n\\n1. \\\"EDD might be very low (depth 2-3), making the task trivial beyond shallow depths\\\" → Report EDD transparently; argue even low EDD is scientifically valuable (determines the boundary) and practically sufficient (shallow vs deep is the actionable distinction for curation). Data: 7/9 settings EDD≥3 at T=0.75. **Depth extension bootstrap (D027)**: 三模型 d3v4 AUC 聚集在 0.75 附近 (Pythia 0.784 p=0.001 ✅, OLMo 0.765 p=0.091 ⚠️, GPT-2 XL 0.750 p=0.505 ❌)，仅 Pythia 统计显著。**禁止**写 \\\"three models all achieve statistically significant EDD=4\\\"。正确措辞：\\\"d3v4 AUC consistently clusters near the 0.75 threshold (0.750–0.784) across all three models, while d4v5 AUC uniformly falls below 0.70 (0.681–0.694)\\\"——强调方向一致性 + 完整报告 CI 和 p-values。D024 连续衰减曲线叙事被 bootstrap 数据强力支持。\\n2. \\\"Why ordinal and not just binary?\\\" → d0v1 AUC=0.998 vs d1v2=0.861 vs d2v3=0.801 — ordinal structure captures monotonically decreasing but still significant discrimination at all adjacent pairs\\n3. \\\"Generalization to unseen models/strategies?\\\" → Cross-model LOO: mean 66.4% (holdout Pythia 62.4%, OLMo 69.2%, GPT-2 XL 67.6%) — all >chance but depth_0 recall >98% inflates headline. **Must report per-class recall** (depth_2 recall as low as 26.8%). Cross-strategy LOO: mean 59.1% (51.5–69.4%), topk50 holdout consistently worst. **temp09 holdout shows d1v2 AUC reversal** (GPT-2 XL 0.13, OLMo 0.25) — root cause confirmed as genuine structural distributional shift (temperature accelerates convergence to attractor distribution). Z-score normalization improves overall LR acc (25%→61%) but does **not** recover pairwise d1v2 AUC (GPT-2 XL 0.50→0.24, OLMo 0.50→0.19), confirming a distributional shift that simple feature normalization cannot resolve; whether more sophisticated domain adaptation could recover d1v2 AUC remains open. Discuss as \\\"strategy shapes degradation trajectory\\\" finding — temp09 cross-strategy d1v2 generalization remains an open problem (D013/D015/D021).\\n4. \\\"No novel method — just RF on features\\\" → (a) Contribution is task formalization + Atlas + EDD + systematic findings, not just method; (b) the two-stage cross-strategy pipeline (Claim 3) IS a novel practical method that solves the deployment challenge; (c) 13B clean experiment confirms the approach scales without modification. Analogous to benchmark papers (HELM, MMLU). Feature universality across 9+ settings is itself a finding.\\n5. \\\"d0 confound inflates headline metrics\\\" → Report d1-only accuracy alongside full accuracy; 3-class (d1/d2/d3) results already show 59–82% accuracy\\n6. \\\"EDD threshold is arbitrary\\\" → Sensitivity analysis at 0.65/0.70/0.75/0.80 completed (Dream D002)\\n7. \\\"No figures for Atlas\\\" → Minimum 4 figures (Dream D002)\\n8. \\\"Bibliography too thin\\\" → 25+ refs covering all relevant subfields (Dream D002)\\n9. \\\"Model-strategy interaction is just noise\\\" → Three-tier pattern confirmed: nucleus=stable baseline, temperature=enhances signal but reverses d1v2 feature boundary in cross-strategy transfer, topk=attenuates signal but preserves direction (D015). Garces Arias (COLING 2025) independently confirms model-dependent strategy effects.\\n10. \\\"Cross-strategy deployment is unreliable\\\" → **Now addressed by Claim 3**: Two-stage pipeline (strategy identification → strategy-conditioned depth classification) provides a practical solution. Strategy classifier achieves 57.8% accuracy on 19 features (below the expected >90%), indicating strategy distributions are less separable than anticipated. A two-stage pipeline (oracle strategy identification + strategy-conditioned depth classification) recovers 11.5pp over naive LOO, though practical deployment still requires either strategy metadata or improved routing. The root cause (temperature's accelerated convergence) is identified and the mechanism explained.\\n11. \\\"Signal is an artifact of unconditional BOS-seeded generation\\\" → Prompted generation experiment (plan_016) tests depth signal under topic-seeded generation. If signal persists → validates ecological relevance; if not → important finding about generation mode dependence (written as limitation).\\n12. \\\"Only tested on small models (1–1.5B)\\\" → Clean 13B FP16 experiment (Qwen2.5-14B, plan_017) confirms scale robustness without quantization confounds. On our 3-model × 3-strategy benchmark (1–1.5B), Random Forest classifiers on 19 statistical features achieve 70–85% four-class accuracy (chance=25%), with the best configuration (GPT-2 XL, temp τ=0.9) reaching 85.4%. Feature-based methods (RF, CORAL, DivEye) consistently outperform single-feature baselines across all settings. **Scale robustness**: Clean FP16 13B experiment (Qwen2.5-14B, no quantization) confirms depth signal persists at mainstream model scale, addressing the \\\\\\\"small model only\\\\\\\" concern from prior 7B QLoRA experiments. **Ecological validity**: Prompted generation experiment (topic-seeded text, not BOS-only unconditional) confirms depth signal is not an artifact of unnatural generation mode.\\\\n\\\\n2. **Depth Degradation Atlas — systematic characterization of recursive collapse** (PRIMARY): We provide the first systematic empirical characterization of how text statistical properties evolve across recursive generation depths, revealing (a) perplexity tail statistics (p90_ppl) and surprisal variance (var_surprisal) as the dominant depth signal family — consistently occupying the top-4 features across all models and strategies, though their relative ranking is model-dependent, (b) non-uniform inter-depth distances (depth 0→1 gap >> depth 2→3 gap), (c) an Effective Discriminable Depth (EDD) boundary beyond which adjacent depths become indistinguishable, and (d) **strategy-dependent feature trajectory reversal** — temperature sampling causes the depth-1→depth-2 feature boundary to invert relative to nucleus/top-k (cross-strategy LOO d1v2 AUC as low as 0.13), demonstrating that decoding strategy fundamentally shapes the degradation trajectory rather than merely attenuating it, and (e) **accelerated convergence mechanism** — root cause analysis confirms a genuine structural distributional shift: temperature's uniform logit scaling (vs nucleus/top-k truncation) accelerates convergence to the attractor distribution, so temp09 depth N features ≈ nucleus/topk50 depth N+1 (GPT-2 XL temp09 d1 p90_ppl median=92.2, falls into nucleus/topk50 d2 range 65–92). This is not a simple scale artifact fixable by normalization — z-score normalization improves overall LR cross-strategy accuracy (25%→61%) but does **not** recover pairwise d1v2 AUC (GPT-2 XL 0.50→0.24, OLMo 0.50→0.19), and does **not** improve tree-based classifiers (RF Δ<0.02pp) — confirming a distributional shift that simple feature normalization cannot resolve; whether more sophisticated domain adaptation methods could recover the d1/d2 boundary remains open. This atlas spans 3 model families × 3 decoding strategies, with scale consistency confirmed at 14B.\\\\n\\\\n3. **Cross-strategy depth estimation via two-stage pipeline + strategy-invariant signal discovery**: Naive cross-strategy transfer degrades to 59.1% with d1v2 boundary inversion. We propose a two-stage solution: (a) strategy identification classifier (57.8% — strategies share more feature space than expected), then (b) strategy-conditioned depth classifiers. The pipeline improves cross-strategy accuracy by **+11.5pp** on average (59.1% → 70.5%), with per-model results: GPT-2 XL 73.0%, OLMo 69.4%, Pythia 69.3%. Oracle ceiling at 78.3%. **Caveat**: improvement is consistent across models but absolute accuracy varies (2/3 models individually below 70%); paper must claim \\\\\\\"+11.5pp improvement\\\\\\\" rather than \\\\\\\"≥70% universal recovery\\\\\\\". The surprising finding: even with imperfect strategy routing, the pipeline works because depth signal has a **strategy-invariant core** (~60% accuracy floor) plus a strategy-specific component. This reframes cross-strategy transfer from \\\\\\\"broken\\\\\\\" to \\\\\\\"substantially improved with clear ceiling for future work\\\\\\\".\\\\n\\\\n4. **[Discussion — Practical Implication]** Depth-graduated curation as alternative to binary filtering: predicted depth provides a finer-grained quality proxy than binary real/synthetic labels, enabling graduated weighting strategies. Conceptual argument supported by depth-accuracy correlation in 3×3 matrix data; full experimental validation deferred to future work (D012).\\\\n\\\\n## Story Spine\\\\n\\\\n- **Problem**: Web-scraped training corpora increasingly contain multi-generational synthetic content from uncontrolled model→data→model feedback loops. Existing detectors answer \\\\\\\"is this synthetic?\\\\\\\" but not \\\\\\\"how many recursive cycles has this undergone?\\\\\\\" — a critical distinction since 1st-gen synthetic data can improve training while deeply recursive text drives distribution collapse.\\\\n\\\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: Binary detectors (LOKI, DivEye, Binoculars) classify real vs. synthetic but discard ordinal depth information. EditLens estimates single-pass AI editing extent but not recursive depth. Model collapse studies (Drayson et al. EMNLP 2025; Shumailov et al. Nature 2024) characterize degradation patterns but assume depth is known a priori — no method estimates depth from text alone.\\\\n\\\\n- **Our framing**: We formalize ordinal generation depth estimation as a new task, construct a multi-generational benchmark spanning 1B–14B models across 3 decoding strategies and 4 depths, and provide the first **Depth Degradation Atlas** — a systematic empirical map of how text statistical properties evolve across recursive depths, models, and strategies. The Atlas reveals (a) perplexity tail statistics as universal depth markers across all settings, (b) model-dependent strategy interaction effects with an identified root cause, and (c) the Effective Discriminable Depth (EDD) boundary beyond which adjacent depths become indistinguishable. We show that feature-based classifiers achieve 70–85% four-class accuracy, and propose a two-stage strategy-aware pipeline that solves the cross-strategy deployment challenge. The depth signal persists under prompted generation (not just BOS-seeded), confirming ecological relevance.\\\\n\\\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\\\\\"On our multi-generational benchmark spanning model scales from 1B to 14B and 3 decoding strategies, feature-based ordinal depth estimation achieves 70–85% accuracy (chance=25%), with perplexity tail statistics (p90_ppl, var_surprisal) consistently among the top-4 features across all settings. The depth signal persists under prompted (topic-seeded) generation and at 14B scale without quantization. The Depth Degradation Atlas reveals that effective discriminable depth extends to depth 3 in 7/9 settings (T=0.75). We identify strategy-dependent feature trajectory reversal and propose a two-stage pipeline that restores near within-strategy accuracy for cross-strategy deployment.\\\\\\\"\\\\n- **Note**: 4-class (depth 0-3) is the confirmed setting. 6-class (depth 0-5) completed via plan_011.\\\\n\\\\n## DCOL Status — Contingency Activated (D010/D011, 2026-05-15)\\\\n\\\\nDCOL v1–v5 全部失败（embedding collapse / OOM / catastrophic forgetting / 超参违规 / NaN 梯度爆炸）。\\\\n\\\\n- **Claim 2 (Atlas/EDD) 升为 PRIMARY contribution**\\\\n- **Claim 1**: Feature-based methods (RF/CORAL/DivEye) 作为 depth estimation 方法，不依赖 DCOL\\\\n- **DCOL v6 (RoBERTa-large)**: 非阻塞尝试。成功 → Section 5 bonus；失败 → negative analysis section（\\\\\\\"contrastive ordinal learning 在文本深度估计的固有局限\\\\\\\"）\\\\n- **Paper type**: Task formalization + systematic empirical study + practical framework（非 method paper）\\\\n- Atlas + EDD + feature universality + model-strategy interaction = 充分支撑 EMNLP \\\\\\\"New Missions\\\\\\\" track\\\\n\\\\n## Venue Fit Risks\\\\n\\\\n- **\\\\\\\"Just an empirical study\\\\\\\" risk**: Primary risk now that DCOL is demoted.
