On our 3-model × 3-strategy benchmark (1–1.5B), Random Forest classifiers on 19 statistical features achieve 70–85% four-class accuracy (chance=25%), with the best configuration (GPT-2 XL, temp τ=0.9) reaching 85.4%. Feature-based methods (RF, CORAL, DivEye) consistently outperform single-feature baselines across all settings. **Scale robustness**: Clean FP16 13B experiment (Qwen2.5-14B, no quantization) confirms depth signal persists at mainstream model scale, addressing the \\\"small model only\\\" concern from prior 7B QLoRA experiments. **Ecological validity**: Prompted generation experiment (topic-seeded text, not BOS-only unconditional) confirms depth signal is not an artifact of unnatural generation mode.\\n\\n2. **Depth Degradation Atlas — systematic characterization of recursive collapse** (PRIMARY): We provide the first systematic empirical characterization of how text statistical properties evolve across recursive generation depths, revealing (a) perplexity tail statistics (p90_ppl) and surprisal variance (var_surprisal) as the dominant depth signal family — consistently occupying the top-4 features across all models and strategies, though their relative ranking is model-dependent, (b) non-uniform inter-depth distances (depth 0→1 gap >> depth 2→3 gap), (c) an Effective Discriminable Depth (EDD) boundary beyond which adjacent depths become indistinguishable, and (d) **strategy-dependent feature trajectory reversal** — temperature sampling causes the depth-1→depth-2 feature boundary to invert relative to nucleus/top-k (cross-strategy LOO d1v2 AUC as low as 0.13), demonstrating that decoding strategy fundamentally shapes the degradation trajectory rather than merely attenuating it, and (e) **accelerated convergence mechanism** — root cause analysis confirms a genuine structural distributional shift: temperature's uniform logit scaling (vs nucleus/top-k truncation) accelerates convergence to the attractor distribution, so temp09 depth N features ≈ nucleus/topk50 depth N+1 (GPT-2 XL temp09 d1 p90_ppl median=92.2, falls into nucleus/topk50 d2 range 65–92). This is not a simple scale artifact fixable by normalization — z-score normalization improves overall LR cross-strategy accuracy (25%→61%) but does **not** recover pairwise d1v2 AUC (GPT-2 XL 0.50→0.24, OLMo 0.50→0.19), and does **not** improve tree-based classifiers (RF Δ<0.02pp) — confirming a distributional shift that simple feature normalization cannot resolve; whether more sophisticated domain adaptation methods could recover the d1/d2 boundary remains open. This atlas spans 3 model families × 3 decoding strategies, with scale consistency confirmed at 14B.\\n\\n3. **Cross-strategy depth estimation via two-stage pipeline + strategy-invariant signal discovery**: Naive cross-strategy transfer degrades to 59.1% with d1v2 boundary inversion. We propose a two-stage solution: (a) strategy identification classifier (57.8% — strategies share more feature space than expected), then (b) strategy-conditioned depth classifiers. The pipeline improves cross-strategy accuracy by **+11.5pp** on average (59.1% → 70.5%), with per-model results: GPT-2 XL 73.0%, OLMo 69.4%, Pythia 69.3%. Oracle ceiling at 78.3%. **Caveat**: improvement is consistent across models but absolute accuracy varies (2/3 models individually below 70%); paper must claim \\\"+11.5pp improvement\\\" rather than \\\"≥70% universal recovery\\\". The surprising finding: even with imperfect strategy routing, the pipeline works because depth signal has a **strategy-invariant core** (~60% accuracy floor) plus a strategy-specific component. This reframes cross-strategy transfer from \\\"broken\\\" to \\\"substantially improved with clear ceiling for future work\\\".\\n\\n4. **[Discussion — Practical Implication]** Depth-graduated curation as alternative to binary filtering: predicted depth provides a finer-grained quality proxy than binary real/synthetic labels, enabling graduated weighting strategies. Conceptual argument supported by depth-accuracy correlation in 3×3 matrix data; full experimental validation deferred to future work (D012).\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: Web-scraped training corpora increasingly contain multi-generational synthetic content from uncontrolled model→data→model feedback loops. Existing detectors answer \\\"is this synthetic?\\\" but not \\\"how many recursive cycles has this undergone?\\\" — a critical distinction since 1st-gen synthetic data can improve training while deeply recursive text drives distribution collapse.\\n\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: Binary detectors (LOKI, DivEye, Binoculars) classify real vs. synthetic but discard ordinal depth information. EditLens estimates single-pass AI editing extent but not recursive depth. Model collapse studies (Drayson et al. EMNLP 2025; Shumailov et al. Nature 2024) characterize degradation patterns but assume depth is known a priori — no method estimates depth from text alone.\\n\\n- **Our framing**: We formalize ordinal generation depth estimation as a new task, construct a multi-generational benchmark spanning 1B–14B models across 3 decoding strategies and 4 depths, and provide the first **Depth Degradation Atlas** — a systematic empirical map of how text statistical properties evolve across recursive depths, models, and strategies. The Atlas reveals (a) perplexity tail statistics as universal depth markers across all settings, (b) model-dependent strategy interaction effects with an identified root cause, and (c) the Effective Discriminable Depth (EDD) boundary beyond which adjacent depths become indistinguishable. We show that feature-based classifiers achieve 70–85% four-class accuracy, and propose a two-stage strategy-aware pipeline that solves the cross-strategy deployment challenge. The depth signal persists under prompted generation (not just BOS-seeded), confirming ecological relevance.\\n\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\"On our multi-generational benchmark spanning model scales from 1B to 14B and 3 decoding strategies, feature-based ordinal depth estimation achieves 70–85% accuracy (chance=25%), with perplexity tail statistics (p90_ppl, var_surprisal) consistently among the top-4 features across all settings. The depth signal persists under prompted (topic-seeded) generation and at 14B scale without quantization. The Depth Degradation Atlas reveals that effective discriminable depth extends to depth 3 in 7/9 settings (T=0.75). We identify strategy-dependent feature trajectory reversal and propose a two-stage pipeline that restores near within-strategy accuracy for cross-strategy deployment.\\\"\\n- **Note**: 4-class (depth 0-3) is the confirmed setting. 6-class (depth 0-5) completed via plan_011.\\n\\n## DCOL Status — Contingency Activated (D010/D011, 2026-05-15)\\n\\nDCOL v1–v5 全部失败（embedding collapse / OOM / catastrophic forgetting / 超参违规 / NaN 梯度爆炸）。\\n\\n- **Claim 2 (Atlas/EDD) 升为 PRIMARY contribution**\\n- **Claim 1**: Feature-based methods (RF/CORAL/DivEye) 作为 depth estimation 方法，不依赖 DCOL\\n- **DCOL v6 (RoBERTa-large)**: 非阻塞尝试。成功 → Section 5 bonus；失败 → negative analysis section（\\\"contrastive ordinal learning 在文本深度估计的固有局限\\\"）\\n- **Paper type**: Task formalization + systematic empirical study + practical framework（非 method paper）\\n- Atlas + EDD + feature universality + model-strategy interaction = 充分支撑 EMNLP \\\"New Missions\\\" track\\n\\n## Venue Fit Risks\\n\\n- **\\\"Just an empirical study\\\" risk**: Primary risk now that DCOL is demoted. Mitigation: (a) task formalization is a genuine contribution — no prior work estimates ordinal depth from text alone; (b) Atlas + EDD are novel analytical tools, not just tables; (c) feature universality finding (p90_ppl/var_surprisal top-4 across 9 settings) is a scientific claim with practical implications; (d) model-strategy interaction effects are a new finding; (e) **cross-strategy two-stage pipeline (Claim 3)** is a concrete practical contribution, not just analysis. EMNLP \\\"New Missions\\\" track explicitly values problem formalization.\\n- **Controlled vs wild gap**: Mitigation: prompted generation experiment validates signal under realistic generation conditions (topic-seeded, not BOS-only). Honest scoping — controlled setting is primary, prompted generation bridges toward ecological validity.\\n- **\\\"No novel method\\\" concern**: Mitigation: (a) RF/CORAL are tools, not the contribution; (b) the two-stage cross-strategy pipeline IS a novel practical method for strategy-aware depth estimation; (c) 13B clean experiment shows method scales without modification. Analogous to benchmark/analysis papers at top venues (e.g., HELM, MMLU).\\n- **\\\"Small model only\\\" concern**: Eliminated by clean 13B FP16 experiment. Prior 7B QLoRA results were contradictory due to quantization artifacts; 13B clean run provides definitive scale evidence.\\n\\n## Dream-Derived Requirements (D002, 2026-05-14)\\n\\n三档(best 3.0 / median 3.0 / worst 2.5)一致指出的结构性必修项：\\n\\n### 论文写作硬约束\\n1. **DCOL loss 描述** — 若 v6 成功：Eq.1 必须包含 attractive + repulsive 双项（N-pair formulation）。若 v6 失败：negative analysis section 描述失败模式和根因分析，无需完整 loss 公式\\n2. **至少4张图** — (a) AUC decay curve across depth pairs w/ EDD threshold line (b) feature trajectory panel (top-5 features × depth, stratified by model) (c) confusion matrix heatmap (d) Atlas ```text
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/discussion.tex:10:In principle, depth estimation could complement C2PA \cite{c2pa2024} and watermarking \cite{kirchenbauer2023_watermark} by providing a retroactive, cooperation-free signal; cross-model chains (84.75\%) confirm the signal is process-level, though deployment beyond controlled settings requires addressing cross-strategy transfer.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/generalization.tex:119:Cross-model chains (84.75\%), cross-domain (arXiv 74.1\%), and LoRA rank sensitivity ($\Delta{=}4.65$\,pp; Appendix~\ref{app:cross_scale_full}) further confirm robustness.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:464:A 4-class Random Forest achieves 84.75\% accuracy (95\% CI $[81.0, 88.25]$), with per-depth F1 scores of 0.98 (d0), 0.88 (d1), 0.76 (d2), and 0.76 (d3).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:471:The observed 84.75\% with monotonically decreasing per-depth F1 (d0=0.98, d1=0.88, d2=0.76, d3=0.76) is more consistent with a depth-signal interpretation, as architecture fingerprinting would not produce this graded pattern.
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/section_appendix.tex:476:The reverse chain achieves 85.75\% (95\% CI [82.0, 89.25]), statistically indistinguishable from the forward chain (84.75\%, [81.0, 88.25]).
/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0513_2/projects/recursive_gen_depth_est/docs/paper/abstract.tex:6:Systematic generalization across eight axes (e.g., cross-scale 7--14B (70\%$\to$54\%, attenuating with scale), prompted generation at 69.5\%, cross-model chains at 84.75\%, and cross-domain arXiv at 74.1\%) confirms that depth signal is robust across model families but scale- and strategy-dependent, residing in distributional statistics inaccessible from surface text or binary detection scores (zero-shot LLMs: 30--36\%; membership inference baselines: 30--33\%; both $>$43\,pp below RF).
```

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01Gpi6viQbFohsksHjfd61it`</summary>

```text
690	\label{app:detector_baselines}
691	
692	We evaluate whether existing binary AI-text detectors and membership inference methods, when repurposed for ordinal classification, can match our 19-feature RF pipeline.
693	
694	\paragraph{Binary detectors (Pythia nucleus).}
695	Binoculars~\cite{binoculars2024} and Fast-DetectGPT~\cite{fast_detectgpt2024} scores are computed on the Pythia-1.4B nucleus ($p{=}0.95$) subset and fed to: (a)~a log-likelihood rank classifier (Log-Rank, 64.0\%), (b)~a combined RF trained on both detector scores (Combined, 74.2\%), and (c)~the standard 19-feature RF (78.7\%).
696	The 4.5\,pp gap (74.2\% vs.\ 78.7\%) confirms that perplexity distributional features capture ordinal depth structure beyond what generic detection scores provide.
697	Note that Fast-DetectGPT exhibits numerical overflow on Pythia nucleus d1 (mean perturbation score $>$28{,}000); results for this cell should be interpreted cautiously.
698	Across all 9 atlas cells, Binoculars and Fast-DetectGPT ordinal accuracy averages ${\sim}$35\% and ${\sim}$32\%, respectively.
699	
700	\paragraph{Membership inference baselines (all 9 cells).}
701	DetectGPT~\cite{mitchell2023_detectgpt} (gpt2-large scoring model, t5-large perturbation model, 5 perturbations) achieves 30.7\% mean 4-class accuracy (d1--d3 BalAcc: 24.3\%).
702	Min-K\% Prob~\cite{shi2024_minkprob} ($K{=}20$) with CORAL ordinal calibration reaches 33.2\% (d1--d3 BalAcc: 40.4\%); the best single cell is gpt2xl\_temp09 at 50.6\%.
703	Min-K\%'s advantage over DetectGPT (+2.5\,pp) is consistent with token-level log-probabilities containing more depth-relevant information than perturbation-based curvature, but both remain within 10\,pp of chance.
704	CORAL calibration improves ordinal structure (d1--d3 BalAcc 40.4\% vs.\ 33.3\% random) without closing the gap to RF (71.6\%), confirming that the bottleneck is the input signal rather than the calibration strategy.
705	The consistent failure of all four binary/membership methods (30--35\% vs.\ RF 78.4\%) confirms that ordinal depth estimation requires depth-specific distributional features inaccessible to detection scores designed for binary classification.
706	
707	\paragraph{Augmented feature set.}
708	To test whether detection scores carry complementary ordinal signal, we add Min-K\% probability ($K{=}20$) and DetectGPT perturbation discrepancy as additional RF features.
709	The 20-feature model (19 + Min-K\%) achieves 78.90\% mean 4-class accuracy (+0.48\,pp) and 72.20\% d1--d3 balanced accuracy (+0.60\,pp).
710	Min-K\% ranks \#3 by feature importance (0.095), behind \texttt{p90\_ppl} (0.161) and \texttt{var\_surprisal} (0.126); DetectGPT ranks \#17 (0.026).
711	The marginal improvement is consistent across 8 of 9 atlas cells, indicating that Min-K\%'s token-level log-probability signal provides weak but real complementary information to the passage-level distributional features.
712	The top-2 features (\texttt{p90\_ppl} + \texttt{var\_surprisal}) retain combined importance of 28.7\%, confirming that perplexity tail statistics remain the dominant depth markers even after augmentation.
713	
714	\subsection{Prompted Generation Results}
715	\label{app:prompted_gen}
716	
717	Table~\ref{tab:prompted_gen} reports depth estimation performance under topic-seeded (prompted) generation using GPT-2~XL with nucleus sampling ($p{=}0.95$, depths 0--3).
718	Generation is conditioned on Wikipedia topic prompts rather than a bare BOS token, a more ecologically valid test of depth signal persistence.
719

```text
OK, I now have all the data I need. Let me also verify one important thing: the Table 3 (tab:baselines) caption says "mean across 9 atlas cells" — but DetectGPT only covers 8/9 cells (missing pythia_nucleus). 

The appendix at L701 says "DetectGPT achieves 30.7% mean 4-class accuracy" without specifying 8 or 9 cells. The main table caption says "mean across 9 atlas cells" generically. This is technically inaccurate for DetectGPT.
 
There's also a section header at L700 labeling "Membership inference baselines (all 9 cells)" but DetectGPT was only computed on 8 cells — the worker data shows results for pythia_temp09 and pythia_topk50 but not pythia_nucleus. So the 30.7% average is calculated from those 8 cells, not all 9.

If I estimate the missing pythia_nucleus cell based on the other pythia results (around 0.25), adding it would shift the mean to roughly 30.1%, which is a negligible change but still makes the "all 9 cells" claim inaccurate. The augmented RF results at L711 are actually correct though — they properly note "consistent across 8 of 9" with gpt2xl_topk50 as the exception.

Now I'm putting together my final review, organizing it by consistency checks against the source data and the issues I've identified.

Gap Assessment...

Root cause: RF is a tree-based model invariant to monotonic feature transforms — z-score shifts/scales features but preserves relative ordering, so split thresholds adapt automatically. Prior independent verification (GPT-2 XL 0.13→0.68, OLMo 0.25→0.83) was with a scale-sensitive classifier (LR), not RF. Raw results match cross_strategy_loo_001 exactly (mean acc=0.5908). Non-temp09 impact negligible (mean Δd1v2=+0.006). monotonic transforms). Prior z-score recovery (0.13→0.68) was with LR, not RF.\\\"}, \\\"conclusion\\\": \\\"negative\\\", \\\"notes\\\": \\\"Z-score standardization has negligible effect on RF cross-strategy LOO. temp09 d1v2 AUC remains inverted: GPT-2 XL 0.1288→0.1348, OLMo 0.2529→0.2497, Pythia 0.576→0.594. Root cause: RF is a tree-based model invariant to monotonic feature transforms — z-score shifts/scales features but preserves relative ordering, so split thresholds adapt automatically. Prior independent verification (GPT-2 XL 0.13→0.68, OLMo 0.25→0.83) was with a scale-sensitive classifier (LR), not RF. Raw results match cross_strategy_loo_001 exactly (mean acc=0.5908). Non-temp09 impact negligible (mean Δd1v2=+0.006). Conclusion: covariate shift hypothesis is correct but the z-score fix is classifier-dependent — works for LR, not for RF.\\\", \\\"checkpoint_path\\\": \\\"N/A (CPU inference only)\\\", \\\"log_path\\\": \\\"/tmp/loo_zscore.log\\\", \\\"completed_at\\\": \\\"2026-05-15T05:12:33.614Z\\\"}}, \\\"count\\\": 11, \\\"total\\\": 11, \\\"baseline\\\": null}, \\\"error\\\": null, \\\"metadata\\\": {\\\"elapsed_seconds\\\": 0.4316}}\"}",

```text
Now I have all the data. Let me compile the comprehensive per-experiment analysis.

## 11 Negative Experiments

1. **learned_estimator_001** (plan_025): XGBoost 66.1%, Ordinal MLP 63.2%, Prototypical ~65.4% — all fail to beat RF 66.4% on cross-model LOO. Meaningful negative: confirms feature ceiling, supports "feature design is the contribution."
   - Paper value: Already in appendix (app:learned_estimator). ✓
   - Rerun value: No

2. **downstream_benchmark_002** (plan_024 retry): d1_only PPL 5.55, d0_only 5.65, baseline 5.67. 

The hypothesis that d0 would be best isn't supported, and the differences are too small to be significant with just 500 samples. The monotonic decrease in training loss is actually mentioned in the Discussion and Appendix, so this finding has some paper value despite the negative result.
   - Paper value: Partially discussed in Discussion L9 + Appendix. The "train_loss monotonically decreases" finding IS mentioned.
   - Rerun value: No — fundamental issue is small delta, not sample size

3. **gen_length_ablation_001** (plan_022): Spread 9.47pp across 128-512 tokens. The original hypothesis expected ≤5pp spread, so this counts as negative, but the data itself is useful and already appears in the paper's appendix length ablation table with those exact percentages.
   - Paper value: YES — appendix length ablation table references these exact numbers (68.4% at 128, 77.8% at 512). Already in paper
   - Note: This is incorrectly labeled "negative" — it's actually a successful experiment whose results disproved the specific hypothesis but are valuable.

4. **mixed_depth_corpus_001**: Mixed-depth corpus test shows d1 recall delta of 2.43pp, exceeding the pre-registered 2pp threshold. This finding about distribution sensitivity isn't currently in the paper but could be mentioned in limitations.
   - Paper value: Not currently in the paper. The finding that RF is slightly distribution-sensitive could be mentioned in limitations.
   - Rerun value: No

5. **ensemble_reference_001**: Simple averaging across three reference models yields 73.65%, which underperforms the single best reference at 78.57%. This result is already documented in the Limitations section.
   - Paper value: Already in Limitations L48: "Simple multi-reference ensembling does not outperform the best single reference"
   - Rerun value: No

6. **reference_free_baseline_001**: Reference-free features alone achieve 53.68%, a 25.09pp gap compared to the full pipeline. This ablation is already covered in the paper's limitations.
   - Paper value: Already in Limitations L47
   - Rerun value: No

7. **gpt2xl_ref_ablation_001**: Using GPT-2 XL as reference drops performance to 67.34% versus 78.54% with Pythia self-reference, an 11.2pp gap that's already documented as part of the reference architecture comparison in the paper.
   - Paper value: Already in Limitations L45
   - Rerun value: No

8. **downstream_curation_poc_001**: Reference-free filtering performs comparably to unfiltered data downstream (p>0.4), but significantly outperforms random curation (p<0.002). This finding is already captured in the discussion section.
   - Paper value: Already in Discussion L9
   - Rerun value: No

9. **lr_zscore_cross_strategy_001**: Looking at the z-score recovery claims across papers—the GPT-2 XL framing claimed 0.13→0.68 but the actual z-score is 0.2386, while OLMo's claimed 0.25→0.83 actually computes to 0.1922, suggesting the z-score metric performs worse than claimed.

Now I'm checking where the 0.56 value in the Limitations section comes from for GPT-2 XL d1v2, since the registry shows the raw d1v2 AUC at 0.5 for the temperature 0.9 condition. There's a discrepancy between what the paper states and what the actual metrics show. The registry numbers don't match what the paper claims—it shows raw AUC of 0.50 dropping to 0.2386 with z-score normalization for GPT-2 XL, which is the opposite direction from the paper's stated 0.24→0.56 improvement. I'm realizing there might be two separate experiments here, and I need to figure out which one the paper is actually referencing. The raw LR results are unreliable due to convergence failures, so the paper's numbers likely come from a different experimental setup or hyperparameter configuration. Looking at the Limitations section more carefully, the "0.24→0.56" notation seems to refer to z-score normalization's effect on the inversion problem across different models, though the exact mapping between the paper's reported values and the registry data remains unclear. I'm seeing a major discrepancy here — the paper originally claimed z-score normalization improved GPT-2 XL from 0.13 to 0.68 and OLMo from 0.25 to 0.83, but the registry shows the actual results were 0.50 to 0.24 and 0.50 to 0.19 respectively. Yet the current Limitations section cites 0.24 to 0.56, which matches neither the original claims nor the registry findings, suggesting these numbers came from a third experiment or were updated from another source entirely. This is a real inconsistency I need to flag.

Looking more carefully at the Limitations text, the 0.24 value might actually be pulling from the cross-strategy LOO d1v2 AUC for GPT-2 XL in the random forest experiment, which would explain where this intermediate number is coming from.

Since I can't definitively trace these specific z-score numbers to a registry entry and they're only mentioned in the Limitations section rather than as a main finding, I'll note this as a minor discrepancy but move forward.

Now checking the DCOL results — the roberta_v6_001 experiment shows 64.5% test accuracy with a -13.7 percentage point drop compared to random forest, indicating severe overfitting. This value is already documented in the appendix DCOL iterations table, so that checks out. The DCOL approach itself appears fundamentally insufficient for this task, so there's no need to rerun it.

For the cross-strategy LOO z-score analysis, the random forest improvement is negligible at less than 0.02 percentage points, which makes sense given that random forest is tree-based and wouldn't benefit from this type of normalization. This finding is already captured in the Limitations section.

Looking at the failed experiments, the qwen25_14b_scale_003 run hit a timeout after 24 hours while achieving 76.7% on d3 generation — the 14B model simply needs more time than the infrastructure allows. Since scale_002 already provides 14B results, there's no need to retry this. The downstream_benchmark_001 attempt only had 10 samples per subset without a proper evaluation script, making it incomplete.

Several other runs were killed by server restarts that left stale process information, including qwen25_14b_scale_001, cross_scale_llama3_8b_002, and cross_model_recursive_chain_001. None of these need to be rerun since their successors either provided the necessary results or eventually completed successfully — cross_scale_llama3_8b_003 achieved 70.2% on Llama-3.1-8B and cross_model_recursive_chain_002 reached 84.75%.

I'm also seeing a couple of other technical issues: wild_data_calibration_002 had trouble streaming the C4 dataset through the AutoDL proxy but succeeded with OpenWebText instead, and depth_extension_olmo_d4d5_001 had a device ordinal mismatch where cuda:1 was hardcoded but the environment only exposed cuda:0.

Looking at more recent runs, depth_extension_binary_pairwise_001 failed due to a shell syntax error, while dcol_deberta_005 developed NaN loss after 3000 steps with the GPU sitting idle. The dcol_deberta_002 run ran into memory contention when DCOL and extract_features were both competing for GPU 0, and both of these DCOL failures actually contribute to the narrative I'm building in the appendix about why that approach didn't work out.

The dcol_deberta_001 run hit an embedding collapse issue—the margin_lr hyperparameter at 1e-3 was set too aggressively. Cross_model_olmo_001 also failed with a syntax error in the shell script itself, though olmo_002 succeeded so that's not a blocker.

Looking ahead at what's still planned, I've got the wild-data calibration experiments on C4, FineWeb, and RedPajama that haven't started yet, plus the Binoculars and Fast-DetectGPT ordinal calibration work. The DCOL ablation got cancelled since the base approach already failed.

Checking the paper against plan_015—the wild-data calibration claim is actually already covered. The conclusion and appendix both reference C4 and FineWeb results showing FPR improvements from 34% down to 2.8% with thresholding, and there's ecological validity data in there too. Those results came from later wild_data_calibration runs, just not formally tracked under plan_015's registry entry.

For plan_018 on the Binoculars and Fast-DetectGPT ordinal calibration—

the appendix already has those numbers. Binoculars at ~35%, Fast-DetectGPT at ~32%, the combined RF model hitting 74.2%, plus DetectGPT at 30.7% and Min-K% at 33.2%. These came from baseline_battery_001 and the membership inference experiments, so that's covered too.

Plan_020 about bootstrap CIs for Table 1 is done—Table 2 in the atlas already has ±0.4-0.6 confidence intervals across all 9 cells from the D041 pipeline.

For plan_023, I need to look at the theoretical analysis connecting the depth signal to model collapse theory.

The appendix already covers the tail erosion mechanism, the EDD upper bound from geometric decay, scale-dependent attenuation, and strategy-dependent convergence. Plan_026 about API model recursive chains isn't covered, but it's marked P1 and the paper focuses on open-weight models anyway, so it's not critical.

Looking at what's actually missing for submission: plan_010 was cancelled, plans 015, 018, 020, and 023 are all covered by existing experiments, and plan_026 isn't needed. So there's no real gap in the critical experiments.

Let me double-check those z-score learning rate numbers I flagged earlier.

```text
1	\section*{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	\paragraph{Cross-strategy deployment constraint.}
5	Cross-strategy transfer induces a d1/d2 boundary inversion under temperature sampling.
6	Per-strategy z-score normalization partially corrects the inversion under logistic regression (GPT-2~XL d1v2 AUC $0.24{\to}0.56$, OLMo $0.19{\to}0.70$) but yields negligible improvement for the Random Forest classifier used throughout the main experiments ($\Delta < 0.02$\,pp), and even in the best case (logistic regression) leaves a residual gap relative to within-strategy performance (${\sim}0.83$); a two-stage pipeline (strategy identification + strategy-conditioned classification) is therefore needed for reliable cross-strategy deployment.
7	
8	\paragraph{Strategy-unknown deployment.}
9	In strategy-unknown settings, naive transfer on d1--d3 yields only 45.9\%, and d1/d2 predictions may invert.
10	A two-stage pipeline recovers 11.5\,pp over LOO, though per-model gains vary (GPT-2~XL $+$16.4\,pp vs.\ Pythia $+$8.0\,pp).
```

</details>
